F-007 · Clarify that the other children wrote Amina’s name

Fixed Low · Content · DE, EN, KY, RU

pair-of-boots — EN: A Pair of Boots; DE: Ein Paar Stiefel; RU: Пара сапог; KY: Бир түгөй өтүк

Issue: Every child participates in the draw, including Amina; four translations then say no child wrote their own name. Azerbaijani explicitly says the other children.

Why it matters: The unqualified statement appears to include Amina. It is possible her own slip was drawn first, but the narrative does not establish that, leaving avoidable ambiguity.

Current check: Approved clarification applied on 3 November 2026: EN, DE, RU and KY now explicitly refer to the other children. All four Word sources, catalogs, search indexes and story HTML were verified; every runtime change is the single approved sentence replacement. Azerbaijani and illustrations were not changed. Word page rendering was unavailable because LibreOffice is not installed. Not deployed.

Evidence and file locations (4 recorded occurrences)

EN · pair-of-boots

en\wisdom-stories\pair-of-boots.docx · en\categories\dostluq-ve-insan-munasibetleri.html#pair-of-boots

Recorded issue text

“Afterwards, I opened all the other slips. Not one child had written their own name. Every single one had written Amina’s—the girl whose family had the least. They all wanted her to win the boots.”

Current local evidence

“Afterwards, I opened all the other slips. None of the other children had written their own name. Every single one had written Amina’s—the girl whose family had the least. They all wanted her to win the boots.”

F-008 · The hidden-cause metaphor ends with awkward English

Fixed Low · Language · EN

windmill-turning-without-wind — EN: A Windmill Turning Without Wind

Issue: The standalone clause “someone is blowing” is grammatically possible but does not naturally convey someone supplying the wind.

Why it matters: The punchline sounds unfinished and distracts from the aphorism.

Current check: 1 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

Evidence and file locations (1 recorded occurrences)

EN · windmill-turning-without-wind

en\wisdom-stories\windmill-turning-without-wind.docx · en\categories\hikmet-ve-heyat-dersleri.html#windmill-turning-without-wind

Recorded issue text

When a windmill turns without wind, someone is blowing.

Current local evidence

If a windmill turns when there is no wind, someone must be making it turn.

No further correction is recommended for the recorded issue. Retain the current verified wording and formatting.

Earlier proposal for context only

Use: “If a windmill turns when there is no wind, someone must be making it turn.” Alternatively preserve the breath image explicitly: “someone must be blowing on it”.

F-009 · The bread metaphor uses an unnatural English collocation

Fixed Low · Language · EN

human-being-emerged — EN: A Human Being Emerged

Issue: “I divided bread with conscience” follows the arithmetic pattern but is not natural English for fair sharing.

Why it matters: Readers may parse conscience as a fellow recipient or as an unexplained mathematical operation.

Current check: 1 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

Evidence and file locations (1 recorded occurrences)

Recorded issue text

I divided bread with conscience—and the result was justice.

Current local evidence

I shared bread according to my conscience—and the result was justice.

No further correction is recommended for the recorded issue. Retain the current verified wording and formatting.

Earlier proposal for context only

Use: “I shared bread according to my conscience—and the result was justice.” Keep the surrounding arithmetic metaphor.

F-010 · Adjacent sentences repeat the same German predicate

Fixed Low · Language · DE

glass-of-milk — DE: Ein Glas Milch

Issue: Two successive sentences use “hatte … nachgelassen”; the third repeats the auxiliary hatte.

Why it matters: The passage is comprehensible but unnecessarily mechanical at an emotional turning point.

Current check: 1 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

F-011 · Two entries retell the same pilgrimage episode

Fixed Low · Cross-story · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

Issue: Both entries use the same pilgrim, self-directed stones, young questioner and inner-purification lesson. One adds a poem and expands the moral.

Current check: Confirmed that hajj-pilgrimage remains in all five languages and stoning-the-devil is absent from the active Word sources, catalogs and corresponding story entries. The old quotation is historical evidence, not a current duplicate.

F-012 · Two entries develop the same heart-distance metaphor

Accepted as is Low · Cross-story · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

keep-your-hearts-close — AZ: Qəlblərinizin ayrılmasına imkan verməyin; EN: Keep your hearts close; DE: Lasst eure Herzen nicht auseinanderdriften; RU: Не позволяйте своим сердцам разлучаться; KY: Жүрөгүңөрдүн алысташына жол бербегиле
why-shout — AZ: Niyə insanlar mübahisə edəndə qışqırırlar?; EN: Why shout?; DE: Warum schreien Menschen, wenn sie streiten?; RU: Почему люди кричат, когда спорят?; KY: Эмне үчүн адамдар урушканда кыйкырышат?

Issue: Both explain shouting through emotional distance and quiet speech through closeness. One is a reflective essay; the other a classroom parable.

Why it matters: Readers may perceive repeated material as a new story unless the relationship is made clear.

Current check: Both stories remain in all five languages. Their shared metaphor is still present, but the user explicitly approved keeping both. This is an accepted editorial choice, not an outstanding fix.

F-013 · English moral generalizes angry speech to all speech

Fixed Low · Translation · EN

nails-lesson — EN: The nail’s lesson

Issue: The moral begins “A word spoken is like a nail”, whereas the source explicitly limits the warning to words spoken in anger. The next clause assumes that every such word is uttered in anger.

Why it matters: The opening generalization is broader than the source and the story’s lesson.

Current check: 1 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

Evidence and file locations (1 recorded occurrences)

EN · nails-lesson

en\wisdom-stories\Etibar Siracsoy\nails-lesson.docx · en\categories\aile-ve-terbiye.html#nails-lesson

Recorded issue text

Moral: A word spoken is like a nail: it is driven in during a moment of anger, but its mark may remain for a long time. That is why we must think before speaking and pause before causing hurt.

Current local evidence

Moral: A word spoken in anger is like a nail: its mark may remain long after the moment has passed. That is why we must think before speaking and pause before causing hurt.

Current Word wording

A word spoken in anger is like a nail: its mark may remain long after the moment has passed. That is why we must think before speaking and pause before causing hurt.

No further correction is recommended for the recorded issue. Retain the current verified wording and formatting.

Earlier proposal for context only

Begin “A word spoken in anger is like a nail: its mark may remain long after the moment has passed.” Retain the existing final sentence.

F-014 · Two translations abbreviate the author’s gardening memory

Fixed Low · Translation · AZ, EN, RU

weeds — AZ: Alaq otları; EN: Weeds; RU: Сорняки

Issue: English and Russian compress the account of turning the soil and crushing or tearing the weeds. German and Kyrgyz retain that sequence. Russian also adds “тяжёлому” to the father’s wish to teach the children to work.

Why it matters: The central lesson survives, but the concrete action and narrative texture of a named author’s recollection change.

Current check: The restored English and Russian gardening passages match the approved replacements in Word, catalogs and category pages. The Azerbaijani comparison passage remains unchanged as intended.

Current local evidence

We had learned to dig the soil. We also did the weeding: with a spade, we turned the soil over, crushing and breaking up the weeds. But they were not so easy to get rid of. One day, Father called us over and explained, “If you want to get rid of the weeds, dig deep with the spade; until you pull out their roots, the job isn’t finished.”

RU · weeds

Он устроил бахчу, а поливать её должны были мы. Вода в нашем колодце была непригодна для питья, поэтому питьевую воду мы носили из колодца в саду нашего соседа, дяди Агасалима.

F-015 · Chess metaphor describes an impossible promotion

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

pawn-and-the-king — AZ: Dəyişən talelər; EN: Changing Fortunes; DE: Wendungen des Schicksals; RU: Превратности судьбы; KY: Тагдырдын бурулуштары

Issue: All versions say a pawn can become a king. FIDE article 3.7.3.3 permits promotion to queen, rook, bishop or knight, not king. The moral clearly concerns social status, so the intended statement may be figurative.

Why it matters: Readers familiar with chess may interpret the analogy as a factual mistake rather than deliberate poetic licence.

Current check: All five current sources are changing-fortunes.docx. The approved human-status wording is present in Word, catalogs and category pages; pawn-and-the-king remains the public identifier. The obsolete pawn-to-king wording is absent. Illustrations were not re-audited in this text review.

plastic-cup — AZ: Keçmiş nazir seminarda; EN: The plastic cup; DE: Ein ehemaliger Minister beim Seminar; RU: Бывший министр на семинаре; KY: Мурдагы министр семинарда

F-019 · The plant’s identity and fifty-year lifespan need clarification

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

mother-of-pearl-flower — AZ: Sədəf çiçəyi (Lunaria annua); EN: Honesty (Lunaria annua); DE: Einjähriges Silberblatt (Lunaria annua); RU: Лунник однолетний (Lunaria annua); KY: Седеп гүлү (Lunaria annua)

Issue: The story describes one plant raised from a bouquet cutting and kept for fifty years. The name is rendered literally as “mother-of-pearl flower” and equivalent forms. Turkish botanical usage associates sedef çiçeği with Lunaria annua, an annual or biennial; the intended Azerbaijani plant identity has not been established.

Why it matters: A literal botanical interpretation may contradict the plot, while replacing the name without confirming the species could introduce a new mistranslation.

Current check: Closed as Fixed at the user’s request. In all five Word sources and rebuilt site versions, the claim that the plant was rooted from a bouquet fifty years ago has been replaced with memories of her husband’s first flowers and habitual care. The precise fifty-year care period now reads “for years” or its natural equivalent. Fifty years of marriage remains unchanged. The approved Lunaria annua identification and synchronized illustration titles are retained. Source-to-site text checks passed in all five languages. Not deployed.

F-026 · The vizier’s final gesture is not explained in his account

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

vizier-and-the-scholar — AZ: Vəzir və alim; EN: The Vizier and the Scholar; DE: Der Wesir und der Gelehrte; RU: Визирь и учёный; KY: Вазир жана окумуштуу

Issue: The vizier produces an egg and flaps his arms like a bird. The scholar interprets both gestures, but the vizier’s own explanation accounts only for the egg and his refusal to share it.

Why it matters: The comic structure otherwise explains each gesture twice; the final arm movement has no corresponding explanation from the vizier.

Current check: Fixed with the user-approved editorial addition in all five Word sources and rebuilt site versions: the vizier flaps like a hen to tell the scholar to ask a hen for an egg and leave his breakfast alone. This explains the gesture from the vizier’s perspective while preserving the scholar’s interpretation. This is an editorial clarification, not recovered source wording. Illustrations unchanged. Not deployed.

Evidence and file locations (6 recorded occurrences)

AZ · vizier-and-the-scholar

az\wisdom-stories\vizier-and-the-scholar.docx · az\categories\iman-ve-meneviyyat.html#vizier-and-the-scholar

Recorded issue text

Üçüncü dəfə alim özünü göstərib yemək yeyirmiş kimi hərəkət etdi. Vəzir dərhal cibindən bir yumurta çıxardı, sonra qollarını quş qanadı kimi çırpdı. Alim gülümsəyərək vəzirin əlindən öpdü. Vəzir də məclisdən ayrıldı.

Current local evidence

— Sonra ovucunu yuxarı tutdu ki, bir qazan plova nə deyirsən? Mən də əlimi üzüaşağı tutdum ki, üstündə qarası da olsa, yeyərəm. Axırda qarnını göstərib dedi ki, yoldan gəlmişəm, acam. Mən də yumurtanı çıxardım ki, vallah, mən səndən də acam, cibimdə də elə bircə yumurta var. Heç istəmə, verə bilməyəcəyəm! Qollarımı da toyuq kimi çırpdım ki, yumurta istəyirsənsə, get toyuqdan istə, mənim səhər yeməyimə göz dikmə!

EN · vizier-and-the-scholar

en\wisdom-stories\vizier-and-the-scholar.docx · en\categories\iman-ve-meneviyyat.html#vizier-and-the-scholar

Recorded issue text

For his third question, the scholar pointed to himself and mimed eating. The vizier immediately took an egg from his pocket and flapped his arms like a bird. Smiling, the scholar kissed the vizier’s hand, and the vizier left the gathering.

Current local evidence

“Then he held his palm upwards to ask, ‘How about a pot of pilaf?’ I turned my hand down to say, ‘I’ll have some if there’s meat and sauce on top.’ Finally, he pointed to his stomach: ‘I’ve come a long way, and I’m hungry.’ So I took out my egg to tell him, ‘Believe me, I’m even hungrier than you, and this is the only egg I have. Don’t ask for it—you’re not getting it!’ Then I flapped my arms like a hen to say, ‘If you want an egg, go and ask a hen. Keep your eyes off my breakfast!’”

DE · vizier-and-the-scholar

de\wisdom-stories\vizier-and-the-scholar.docx · de\categories\iman-ve-meneviyyat.html#vizier-and-the-scholar

Recorded issue text

Beim dritten Mal zeigte der Gast auf sich und tat so, als würde er essen. Der Wesir holte sofort ein Ei aus seiner Tasche und schlug mit den Armen wie ein Vogel mit den Flügeln. Lächelnd küsste der Gelehrte ihm die Hand. Daraufhin verließ der Wesir die Versammlung.

Current local evidence

„Dann hielt er die Handfläche nach oben: Was hältst du von einem Topf Pilaw? Ich drehte meine Hand nach unten: Wenn Fleisch mit Soße obendrauf kommt, esse ich mit. Zum Schluss zeigte er auf seinen Bauch: Ich habe eine lange Reise hinter mir und bin hungrig. Da holte ich mein Ei heraus: Glaub mir, ich bin noch hungriger als du, und das ist mein einziges Ei. Frag gar nicht erst — das gebe ich nicht her! Dann schlug ich mit den Armen wie ein Huhn mit den Flügeln, um ihm zu sagen: Wenn du ein Ei willst, frag eine Henne und lass mein Frühstück in Ruhe!“

Тут я достал яйцо: поверь, я ещё голоднее тебя, а в кармане у меня всего одно яйцо. Даже не проси — не отдам! А руками замахал, как курица крыльями: хочешь яйцо — иди проси у курицы, а на мой завтрак не зарься!

F-029 · The two predictions are not strictly equivalent

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

fallen-teeth — AZ: Tökülən dişlər; EN: Teeth falling out; DE: Der Traum des Königs; RU: Выпавшие зубы; KY: Түшкөн тиштер

Issue: The narrator says both interpretations have the same meaning. One predicts that all loved ones will die before the king; the other says he will have the longest life in his family. Loved ones and family are not necessarily the same group, and greatest lifespan is not necessarily the latest death when ages differ.

Why it matters: The parable’s central claim of identical information depends on an ambiguity, even though its intended lesson about tact is clear.

Current check: Fixed with user-approved wording in all five Word sources and rebuilt site versions. Both predictions now refer to the same group of loved ones or relatives: they die before the king, and he outlives them. The narrator contrasts their emotional emphasis without confusing lifespan with survival order. The moral and illustrations remain unchanged. Not deployed.

F-031 · Translations turn an absent father into a deceased father

Fixed Low · Translation · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

everyone-is-equal-here — AZ: Bizdə hamı eynidir; EN: Everyone Is Equal Here; DE: Bei uns zählt jeder gleich; RU: У нас все равны; KY: Бизде баары бирдей

Issue: Azerbaijani says the boy grows up without a father (atasız böyüyür); all four translations explicitly state that his father died. The source does not establish the cause of the father’s absence.

Why it matters: The translations add a definite biographical fact that the Azerbaijani text leaves open.

Current check: Fixed as approved: EN, DE, RU and KY Word sources and rebuilt site versions now say the boy is growing up without his father, without asserting that his father died. The Azerbaijani source wording is unchanged. The translations preserve the uncertainty in the original. Illustrations unchanged. Not deployed.

F-039 · Three titles call an underspecified question meaningless

question-the-question — AZ: Sualın əsasını yoxla; DE: Eine sinnlose Frage; RU: Бессмысленный вопрос; KY: Маанисиз суроо

Why it matters: An underspecified question is not necessarily meaningless; these titles weaken the distinction that the story now teaches.

Artwork and morals preserved outside edited text areas. Not deployed.

F-040 · Animal instinct is used as evidence about ethical character

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

education-or-character — AZ: Təhsil, yoxsa xasiyyət?; EN: Education or Character?; DE: Bildung oder Charakter?; RU: Образование или характер; KY: Билимби же мүнөзбү?

Issue: The cat abandons trained behavior to chase a mouse. The conclusion moves from that instinctive action to human honesty, responsibility and strength of character. These are different meanings of character/nature.

Why it matters: The moral is understandable as an analogy, but the episode does not establish that education matters less than ethical character; it illustrates instinct overriding training.

Current check: Fixed with the approved ending and moral in all five Word sources and rebuilt site versions. The cat episode and dialogue remain intact. The ending distinguishes animal instinct from human ethical choice; the moral presents education and character as complementary. Illustration text was not changed in this text revision. Not deployed.

EN · education-or-character

en\wisdom-stories\education-or-character.docx · en\categories\exlaq-ve-xarakter.html#education-or-character

Recorded issue text

Before answering, the vizier released a little mouse he had brought with him. The moment the cat saw it, it dropped the tray and raced after it.

Current local evidence

The king fell silent. The cat’s hunting instinct had overridden its training. But this incident could not serve as a measure of human character: people can reflect on their desires, change their behavior, and take responsibility for their choices. Moral: Knowing how to behave well is not the same as putting that knowledge into practice. What matters is being able to apply what we have learned even in difficult moments. Education and character should complement each other, rather than be set against each other.

Current local evidence

Der König schwieg. Der Jagdinstinkt der Katze hatte sich gegen das antrainierte Verhalten durchgesetzt. Doch daraus ließ sich kein Urteil über den menschlichen Charakter ableiten: Menschen können ihre Wünsche hinterfragen, ihr Verhalten ändern und Verantwortung für ihre Entscheidungen übernehmen. Moral: Zu wissen, wie man sich richtig verhält, heißt noch nicht, auch danach zu handeln. Entscheidend ist, das Gelernte auch in schwierigen Momenten anzuwenden. Bildung und Charakter sollten nicht gegeneinander ausgespielt werden, sondern einander ergänzen.

Увазир жооп берерден мурун өзү менен ала келген кичинекей чычканды жерге коё берди. Мышык аны көрөрү менен табакты таштап, артынан чуркады.

Current local evidence

Падыша унчукпай калды. Мышыктын аңчылык инстинкти үйрөтүлгөн жүрүм-турумунан күчтүү болуп чыкты. Бирок бул окуяга карап адамдын мүнөзүнө баа берүүгө болбойт: адам өз каалоолорун таразалап, жүрүм-турумун өзгөртүп, тандоосу үчүн жоопкерчилик ала алат. Сабак: Өзүн туура алып жүрүүнүн эрежелерин билүү — аларды аткаруу дегендик эмес. Эң негизгиси — үйрөнгөнүбүздү кыйын учурда да колдоно билүү. Билим менен мүнөз бири-бирине каршы коюлбай, бири-бирин толукташы керек.

F-041 · Two titles prohibit complaint rather than encourage reflection

Fixed Low · Translation · AZ, KY, RU

every-day-has-meaning — AZ: Hər günün bir dərsi var; RU: Никогда не жалуйтесь ни на один прожитый день; KY: Жашаган бир да күнүңүзгө нааразы болбоңуз

Issue: RU and KY titles tell readers never to complain about any day they have lived. The body instead says not to dismiss a day as wasted and allows time and self-kindness to understand hardship. AZ title refers to each day’s lesson.

Why it matters: The titles impose a stronger emotional demand than the source and the qualified moral.

Current check: Fixed: approved RU “У каждого дня свой урок” and KY “Ар бир күндүн өз сабагы бар” titles applied to Word sources, site titles and labels, and illustration headers. Azerbaijani text and every-day-has-meaning identifier unchanged. Artwork and morals outside the title panels are pixel-identical. Not deployed.

F-042 · The conclusion blames missing follow-up although inquiries were made

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

silent-corridor — AZ: Səssiz dəhliz; EN: The Silent Corridor; DE: Der schweigende Flur; RU: Тихий коридор; KY: Жымжырт коридор

Issue: Paragraph 5 says inquiries to the sponsor received vague answers. The closing criticism then says the team should have followed up, without specifying how its actual inquiries were insufficient.

Why it matters: Responsibility shifts from evasive communication to a seemingly absent action that the story has already shown. The intended lesson about effective follow-up becomes unclear.

Current check: Fixed with the approved closing passage and moral in all five Word sources and rebuilt site versions. The ending acknowledges the inquiries already made, calls for clear acceptance status and earlier direct contact with responsible staff, and states the sponsor’s duty to communicate rejection promptly. All five active illustration moral panels now reproduce the revised Word moral without its label. Text fit was visually checked; pixels outside the moral text areas are unchanged. Not deployed.

Evidence and file locations (10 recorded occurrences)

AZ · silent-corridor

az\wisdom-stories\Bakhtiyar Sirajov\silent-corridor.docx · az\categories\soz-sukut-ve-unsiyyet.html#silent-corridor

Recorded issue text

Heç kimin dəqiq məlumatı yox idi. Sponsora edilən müraciətlər isə yalnız üstüörtülü, qeyri-müəyyən cavablar gətirirdi.

Current local evidence

«Biz müraciət etmişdik, amma qeyri-müəyyən cavablarla kifayətlənməməliydik. Sənədlərin qəbul olunub-olunmadığına dair aydın cavab istəməli, cavab ala bilməyəndə isə məsələni daha tez məsul şəxslərlə görüşdə aydınlaşdırmalıydıq. Sponsor da sənədlərin niyə qəbul edilmədiyini bizə vaxtında bildirməli idi. Onda nöqsanları daha tez aradan qaldıra, aylarla gözləməkdən qurtula bilərdik. Bu qeyri-müəyyənlik vaxtımızı aldı, inamımızı sarsıtdı.» İbrət: Əməkdaşlıqda yalnız müraciət etmək kifayət deyil; sualların aydın cavabını almaq üçün də çalışmaq lazımdır. Cavab verən tərəf isə qərarını və onun səbəbini vaxtında bildirməlidir. Qeyri-müəyyənlik uzananda məsələni məsul şəxslərlə birbaşa aydınlaşdırmaq vacibdir. Bəzən əməkdaşlığa ən böyük zərəri uğursuzluq yox, səssizlik vurur.

EN · silent-corridor

en\wisdom-stories\Bakhtiyar Sirajov\silent-corridor.docx · en\categories\soz-sukut-ve-unsiyyet.html#silent-corridor

Recorded issue text

No one knew. Inquiries to the sponsor brought only vague, evasive answers.

Current local evidence

“We did make inquiries, but we should not have settled for vague answers. We should have asked for a clear answer about whether the documents had been accepted. When no clear answer came, we should have met with those responsible sooner to resolve the matter. The sponsor, too, should have told us promptly why the documents had not been accepted. We could then have corrected the shortcomings sooner and avoided months of waiting. This uncertainty cost us time and shook our trust.” Moral: In a partnership, making inquiries is not enough; we must also seek clear answers. The responding party must communicate its decision and the reasons for it promptly. When uncertainty persists, it is important to clarify the matter directly with those responsible. Sometimes the greatest harm to a partnership comes not from failure, but from silence.

DE · silent-corridor

de\wisdom-stories\Bakhtiyar Sirajov\silent-corridor.docx · de\categories\soz-sukut-ve-unsiyyet.html#silent-corridor

Recorded issue text

Niemand wusste Genaueres. Auch Nachfragen beim Förderer brachten nur ausweichende, unklare Antworten.

Current local evidence

„Wir hatten nachgefragt, hätten uns aber nicht mit unklaren Antworten zufriedengeben dürfen. Wir hätten eine eindeutige Auskunft darüber verlangen müssen, ob die Unterlagen angenommen worden waren. Als diese ausblieb, hätten wir die Angelegenheit früher in einem Gespräch mit den Verantwortlichen klären sollen. Auch der Förderer hätte uns rechtzeitig mitteilen müssen, warum die Unterlagen nicht angenommen worden waren. Dann hätten wir die Mängel früher beheben können und nicht monatelang warten müssen. Diese Ungewissheit hat uns Zeit gekostet und unser Vertrauen erschüttert.“ Lehre: In einer Zusammenarbeit genügt es nicht, nur nachzufragen; man muss sich auch um klare Antworten bemühen. Die andere Seite wiederum muss ihre Entscheidung und deren Gründe rechtzeitig mitteilen. Hält die Ungewissheit an, ist es wichtig, die Angelegenheit direkt mit den Verantwortlichen zu klären. Manchmal schadet einer Zusammenarbeit das Schweigen mehr als ein Misserfolg.

RU · silent-corridor

ru\wisdom-stories\Bakhtiyar Sirajov\silent-corridor.docx · ru\categories\soz-sukut-ve-unsiyyet.html#silent-corridor

Recorded issue text

Ни у кого не было чёткой информации. На запросы спонсор давал лишь расплывчатые ответы.

Current local evidence

«Мы обращались к спонсору, но не должны были довольствоваться расплывчатыми ответами. Нужно было добиваться ясного ответа: приняты документы или нет. А когда получить такой ответ не удалось, следовало раньше встретиться с ответственными лицами и прояснить ситуацию. Спонсор тоже должен был вовремя сообщить нам, почему документы не приняли. Тогда мы могли бы раньше устранить недостатки и избежать многомесячного ожидания. Эта неопределённость отняла у нас время и подорвала доверие». Мораль: В сотрудничестве недостаточно лишь направлять запросы — нужно стремиться получить ясные ответы. Отвечающая сторона, в свою очередь, должна своевременно сообщать своё решение и его причины. Если неопределённость затягивается, важно прояснить вопрос непосредственно с ответственными лицами. Иногда самый большой вред сотрудничеству наносит не неудача, а молчание.

F-044 · Three titles deny losing instead of encouraging learning from loss

Needs decision Low · Translation · AZ, DE, KY, RU

learning-from-loss — AZ: İtkidən də öyrənmək olar; DE: „Ich habe verloren“ gibt es nicht; RU: Нет такого понятия — «я проиграл»; KY: «Утулдум» деген нерсе жок

Issue: DE, RU and KY titles say there is no such thing as losing. The Azerbaijani title and all bodies acknowledge setbacks and explain that learning can still result.

Why it matters: The absolute titles overstate the qualified message and differ from the source title.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

Evidence and file locations (4 recorded occurrences)

AZ · learning-from-loss

az\wisdom-stories\learning-from-loss.docx · az\categories\hikmet-ve-heyat-dersleri.html#learning-from-loss

Recorded issue text

F-045 · Russian version adds a completed divorce

Fixed Low · Translation · AZ, RU

hayats-life-story — AZ: Həyatın həyat hekayəsi; RU: История жизни Хаят

Issue: The Russian text calls Hayat’s mother divorced, while the source says she left her husband; no divorce is established in the narrative.

Why it matters: This adds a specific change of marital status to the plot.

Current check: 1 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

F-046 · One repeated refrain is split into two paragraphs

Needs decision Low · Structure · AZ

pandemic-and-quarantine — AZ: Pandemiya, karantin

Issue: The second occurrence of the repeated refrain is split after “pandemiya,”, leaving “karantin?” as a separate paragraph. The other occurrences use one paragraph.

Why it matters: The extra paragraph interrupts the recurring stanza structure and creates inconsistent spacing.

Current check: The second refrain still ends one Word/site paragraph with “Niyə gəldin, pandemiya,” and starts the next with “karantin?”. Other occurrences remain on one paragraph. The structural difference is confirmed.

F-048 · Add a source note for the lion and disabled fox parable

Needs decision Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

hands-at-work-hope-in-god — AZ: Əlin işdə, ümidin Allahda; EN: Hands at Work, Hope in God; DE: Die Hände bei der Arbeit, die Hoffnung bei Gott; RU: Руки — за работой, надежда — на Бога; KY: Колуң иште, үмүтүң Аллахта болсун

Issue: The distinctive sequence of a disabled fox fed by a lion, a man imitating its inactivity, and an instruction to emulate the provider occurs in Saadi’s Bustan, chapter II. The current versions omit this identifiable literary parallel.

Why it matters: Readers cannot identify the literary tradition or distinguish this modern retelling from an original account. The evidence establishes a parallel, not the exact route of adaptation.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-049 · The planning analogy treats skill as mere chance

Needs decision Low · Content · AZ, DE, KY, RU

without-a-plan — AZ: Fəaliyyət planı olmayan təşkilat; DE: Eine Organisation ohne Arbeitsplan; RU: Организация без плана действий; KY: Иш-аракет планы жок уюм

Issue: The first paragraph allows a ship to arrive thanks to a capable captain, then classifies that arrival as a result of chance. The Azerbaijani, German, Russian and Kyrgyz versions retain this inference; English already avoids the strongest formulation.

Why it matters: A deliberate response by a skilled captain is not necessarily accidental. The overstatement weakens an otherwise useful distinction between a repeatable plan and improvised success.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

Evidence and file locations (4 recorded occurrences)

AZ · without-a-plan

az\wisdom-stories\Bakhtiyar Sirajov\without-a-plan.docx · az\categories\emek-ruzi-ve-servet.html#without-a-plan

Recorded issue text

Fəaliyyət planı olmayan təşkilat təyinat limanı bəlli olsa da, dəniz xəritəsi, hərəkət marşrutu və ekipaj üzvlərinin vəzifələri müəyyənləşdirilməmiş gəmiyə bənzəyir. Belə gəmi bəzən bəxti gətirdiyi, kapitanı bacarıqlı olduğu və ya hava şəraiti əlverişli keçdiyi üçün limana çata bilər. Ancaq bu, sistemli idarəetmənin deyil, təsadüfün nəticəsi olar. Davamlı uğur isə təsadüflərlə deyil, düzgün planlaşdırılmış və intizamla həyata keçirilən fəaliyyətlə qazanılır.

Current local evidence

Belə gəmi bəzən bəxti gətirdiyi, kapitanı bacarıqlı olduğu və ya hava şəraiti əlverişli keçdiyi üçün limana çata bilər. Ancaq bu, sistemli idarəetmənin deyil, təsadüfün nəticəsi olar. DE · without-a-plan

de\wisdom-stories\Bakhtiyar Sirajov\without-a-plan.docx · de\categories\emek-ruzi-ve-servet.html#without-a-plan

Recorded issue text

Eine Organisation ohne Arbeitsplan gleicht einem Schiff, dessen Zielhafen zwar bekannt ist, für das aber keine Seekarte vorliegt und weder die Route noch die Aufgaben der Besatzungsmitglieder festgelegt sind. Ein solches Schiff kann den Hafen manchmal erreichen, weil es Glück hat, sein Kapitän tüchtig ist oder das Wetter günstig bleibt. Doch das wäre ein Ergebnis des Zufalls, nicht einer systematischen Führung. Dauerhafter Erfolg entsteht durch sorgfältig geplantes und diszipliniert ausgeführtes Handeln, nicht durch Zufälle.

Current local evidence

RU · without-a-plan

Recorded issue text

Организация, у которой нет плана действий, похожа на корабль с известным портом назначения, но без морской карты, проложенного маршрута и распределённых обязанностей между членами экипажа. Иногда такой корабль может достичь порта благодаря удаче, умелому капитану или благоприятной погоде. Однако это будет результатом стечения обстоятельств, а не системного управления. Устойчивый успех достигается не благодаря случайности, а благодаря продуманным планам и дисциплинированным действиям.

F-050 · Equal time allocation can be mistaken for equal lifespans

Needs decision Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY

life-flows-silently — AZ: Zaman — səssiz axan ömür; EN: Life flows silently; DE: Zeit – das Leben, das lautlos dahinfließt; KY: Убакыт — үнсүз аккан өмүр

Issue: Four versions describe time as given to everyone in equal measure. Russian more carefully says the measure of time is the same for everyone.

Why it matters: In a reflection about the length of life, the wording can imply that everyone receives the same lifespan or usable time, rather than that time passes without regard to status.

Current check: The equal-measure claim remains. The English apostrophe is now typographic following F-077; that punctuation change does not resolve the lifespan ambiguity.

Evidence and file locations (4 recorded occurrences)

AZ · life-flows-silently

az\wisdom-stories\Etibar Siracsoy\life-flows-silently.docx · az\categories\yaslanma-ve-zaman.html#life-flows-silently

Recorded issue text

Zaman heç kim üçün dayanmır. Nə var-dövlətə, nə şöhrətə, nə də insanın istəyinə boyun əyir. O, hamıya eyni ölçüdə verilən, lakin hər kəs tərəfindən fərqli qiymətləndirilən ən böyük sərvətdir. İnsanın ömrü, əslində, illərlə deyil, yaşadığı mənalı anlarla ölçülür.

Current local evidence

F-051 · Three titles add a promise about longevity

Needs decision Low · Translation · DE, KY, RU

living-well — DE: Das Geheimnis eines guten und langen Lebens; RU: Секрет хорошей и долгой жизни; KY: Жакшы жашоонун жана узак өмүр сүрүүнүн сыры

Issue: German, Russian and Kyrgyz call this a secret of a good and long life. Azerbaijani and English refer only to living well, and the body offers figurative lifestyle advice rather than a claim about lifespan.

Why it matters: The titles introduce an additional meaning and make the advice sound more medically conclusive than the source.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-052 · English turns the figurative uncle into a definite relative

Needs decision Low · Translation · AZ, EN

credit-ledger — EN: The Credit Ledger; AZ: Nisyə dəftəri

Issue: The English shopkeeper says the girl’s mother’s brother came by. The visitor is introduced as an unknown benefactor; the source echoes the girl’s word “uncle” as a figurative role, without establishing that he is her actual relative.

Why it matters: The explicit kinship assertion changes the ending and can make readers infer that the anonymous donor was the expected relative.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

Recommended choice: Prefer “someone has already paid her debt” for maximum clarity; retain the figurative “uncle” only if preserving that affectionate echo is important to the author.

F-053 · Identify the traditional fable behind the cat’s bell

Needs decision Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

belling-the-cat — AZ: Pişiyin boynundan asılacaq zınqırov; EN: Belling the Cat; DE: Wer hängt der Katze das Glöckchen um?; RU: Колокольчик на шею коту; KY: Мышыктын мойнуна тагыла турган коңгуроо

Issue: The sequence matches the traditional “Belling the Cat” fable included in Aesopic collections. No source or traditional-fable credit appears in the current versions.

Why it matters: Readers lose an identifiable literary reference and cannot distinguish adaptation from an original story.

F-055 · Emotional care is contrasted with medicine as a life-prolonging remedy

Needs decision Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

thoughtfulness — AZ: Diqqət; EN: Thoughtfulness; DE: Zuwendung; RU: Внимание; KY: Көңүл буруу

Issue: All five versions of Thoughtfulness say that sometimes life is prolonged by caring words rather than medicine. The passage is literary, but its contrast can also be read as a literal health claim. Public-health evidence supports the importance of social connection; it does not establish this either/or claim.

Why it matters: The contrast distracts from the supported message about emotional support and can suggest that care substitutes for treatment. The author should decide whether to retain the metaphor or remove that implication.

Current check: The medicine-versus-care contrast remains in all five versions. The English apostrophe correction is present but does not address this interpretive issue. This review does not make a medical recommendation.

F-057 · Hope is expressed as a guarantee that every heartfelt wish will be fulfilled

Needs decision Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

dreaming — AZ: Xəyal qurmaq; EN: Dreaming; DE: Träumen; RU: Мечты; KY: Кыялдануу

Issue: The opening and moral in all five versions present fulfilment of a heartfelt wish as certain. Unlike a general encouragement to hope, the wording leaves no room for an unfulfilled wish. This is an authorial faith statement, not a factual claim this audit can adjudicate.

Why it matters: Readers may understand the encouragement as a promise of a particular outcome. The intended meaning needs clarification before an editor can soften it without changing the author’s religious message.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-058 · The moral praises a benefit without addressing the false offer that produces it

Needs decision Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

butter-price-war — AZ: Baqqal dükanı və supermarket; EN: The butter price war; DE: Der Preiskampf; RU: Продуктовый магазин и супермаркет; KY: Азык-түлүк дүкөнү жана супермаркет

Issue: In all five versions, the shopkeeper advertises butter she does not sell to induce a competitor to lower its price. The moral celebrates helping others but does not acknowledge that the advertised offer is false.

Why it matters: The comic trick and the stated lesson pull in different directions: a reader could take the moral as approval of misleading others whenever the result benefits someone. This is an interpretive tension, not a request to remove humour or a legal conclusion.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-059 · A literary animal analogy reads as an observation of animal behaviour

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

before-you-marry — AZ: Ailə qurmağa hazırlaşanlara tövsiyələr; EN: Before you marry; DE: Ratschläge für eine glückliche Ehe; RU: Советы вступающим в брак; KY: Үй-бүлө курууга камынгандарга кеңеш

Issue: All five versions of Before You Marry describe spiny animals finding a warming distance without identifying this as a parable. The recognisable literary parallel is Schopenhauer’s porcupine parable in Parerga und Paralipomena, II, chapter 31, §396.

Why it matters: The lesson about personal boundaries is clear, but readers are not told that the animal scene is a literary analogy. The text also uses hedgehogs whereas the cited German original uses porcupines.

Current check: 5 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

Evidence and file locations (5 recorded occurrences)

F-063 · Four Azerbaijani verb forms incorrectly join vaz to keçmək

Fixed Low · Language · AZ

4 affected stories and current titles
  • learning-to-say-yet — AZ: Düşüncəni dəyiş, yenidən cəhd et
  • make-time-for-people — AZ: Vaxtını insanlarla keçir
  • price-of-belonging — AZ: Çoxluq həmişə haqlı deyil
  • red-dress — AZ: Qırmızı don

Issue: Four passages use vazkeçmək, vazkeçim, vazkeçdim or vazkeçir as one word. The Azerbaijani orthographic and explanatory dictionaries list the verbal expression as vaz keçmək; other stories already use the spaced form.

Why it matters: The joined spellings introduce orthographic errors and inconsistent spelling of the same expression across the collection.

Current check: 4 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

No further correction is recommended for the recorded issue. Retain the current verified wording and formatting.

Earlier proposal for context only

F-064 · The girl’s line has a redundant second speech tag

Fixed Low · Language · AZ, KY

credit-ledger — AZ: Nisyə dəftəri; KY: Карыз дептери

Issue: The Azerbaijani and Kyrgyz narration introduces the girl’s speech and then redundantly closes the same line with another speech tag. No intervening speaker or action makes the second tag necessary.

Why it matters: The repetition makes a simple exchange feel mechanically narrated and interrupts its flow.

Current check: 2 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

Earlier proposal for context only

AZ: keep “Çəkinə-çəkinə dedi:” in the introduction; end the spoken line after “pulunu verərik.” Remove “— dedi qız.” and start “Dükançı qızın sözlərindən…” as a new narrative paragraph. KY: keep “Ал тартынып сүйлөдү:” and remove “— деди кыз.” after “акчасын төлөйбүз,” changing that comma to a full stop. Start “Кыздын сөзүн уккан дүкөнчү…” as the next narrative paragraph.

F-065 · The opening repeats worn unnecessarily

Fixed Low · Language · EN

red-dress — EN: The Red Dress

Issue: “Among the faded, worn clothes she had worn for years” repeats worn in adjacent descriptive and verbal uses.

Why it matters: The repetition makes the opening sentence heavier without adding useful information.

Current check: 1 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

Evidence and file locations (1 recorded occurrences)

EN · red-dress

en\wisdom-stories\red-dress.docx · en\categories\sevgi-ve-evlilik.html#red-dress

Recorded issue text

The red dress hung in my mother’s wardrobe. Among the faded, worn clothes she had worn for years, it seemed to glow.

Current local evidence

The red dress hung in my mother’s wardrobe. Among the faded clothes she had worn for years, it seemed to glow.

No further correction is recommended for the recorded issue. Retain the current verified wording and formatting.

Earlier proposal for context only

Use: “Among the faded clothes she had worn for years, it seemed to glow.” This preserves the contrast between the old clothing and the red dress.

Issue: 55 paragraphs omit commas at clause boundaries. Most are initial expanded infinitive clauses; two are clauses introduced by ohne. These were checked individually against the 2024 rules, including the exceptions for unextended infinitives and multi-part predicates.

Why it matters: The punctuation does not consistently follow the current German spelling rules and obscures clause boundaries, particularly in longer morals.

Current check: All 55 stored corrected passages match the current Word sources, catalogs and category text after normalizing the Word “Moral:” / site “Lehre:” label and layout whitespace. No additional comma changes are proposed.

F-069 · Cumalı’s poem excerpt is retitled without identifying the original poem

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

sunlit-day — AZ: Heç nəyə dəyişmərəm; EN: A sunlit day; DE: Ich würde ihn gegen nichts eintauschen; RU: Ни на что не променяю; KY: Эч нерсеге алмаштырбайм

Issue: The passage credited to Necati Cumalı corresponds to the latter part of Güneş Delisi. All five versions use local titles and a bare author credit, without identifying the original poem, the excerpt status or the translator/adaptor.

Why it matters: Readers cannot readily locate the original or distinguish a selected translated passage from the complete poem under its original title.

Current check: The author line in all five languages names Güneş Delisi and says the passage is a translated excerpt. The title, identifier, and moral stay separate. No translator or edition was added, because none is recorded.

F-070 · Closing reflection paraphrases the Serenity Prayer without identification

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

what-changed-after-sixty — AZ: Altmışdan sonra nə dəyişdi?; EN: What Changed After Sixty; DE: Was sich nach dem sechzigsten Geburtstag verändert hat; RU: Что изменилось после шестидесяти; KY: Алтымыштан кийин эмне өзгөрдү?

Issue: The final reflection in all five versions follows the distinctive three-part sequence of the Serenity Prayer: changing what is possible, accepting what is not, and distinguishing the two. It is presented as the narrator’s reflection without a source note.

Why it matters: A recognizable borrowed prayer is difficult to trace, unlike the explicitly credited quotations elsewhere in the collection.

Current check: A short note in all five languages identifies the closing lines as a free rendering of the Serenity Prayer, commonly attributed to Reinhold Niebuhr. The narrator’s words, the title, and the moral are unchanged. It is not presented as a verbatim quotation or given a composition date.

Evidence and file locations (5 recorded occurrences)

Current local evidence

Və nəhayət, Rəbbimdən dəyişdirə biləcəklərim üçün güc, dəyişdirə bilməyəcəklərim üçün səbir, bunları bir-birindən ayırmaq üçün isə hikmət diləməyi öyrənmişəm. İnsan olduğumu, hər şeyə gücüm çatmadığını qəbul edirəm. Bu sözlər Reynhold Nibura aid edilən Serenity Prayer duasının sərbəst ifadəsidir.

F-071 · German proverb mixes incompatible comparison structures

Fixed Low · Language · DE

lambs-skins — DE: Die Häute der Lämmer

Issue: The repeated proverb combines the finite clause “man schert die Widder” with the infinitive clause “als den Lämmern die Haut abzuziehen”.

Why it matters: The two halves of the comparison do not connect smoothly; the opening and concluding quotation repeat the same awkward construction.

Current check: 2 recorded replacement passage(s) verified in the current Word sources, catalogs and generated category text. Unchanged comparison passages are not treated as failed corrections.

Evidence and file locations (2 recorded occurrences)

DE · lambs-skins

de\wisdom-stories\lambs-skins.docx · de\categories\edalet-ve-cemiyyet.html#lambs-skins

Recorded issue text

„Besser, man schert die Widder, als den Lämmern die Haut abzuziehen.“

Current local evidence

„Es ist besser, die Widder zu scheren, als den Lämmern die Haut abzuziehen.“

DE · lambs-skins

de\wisdom-stories\lambs-skins.docx · de\categories\edalet-ve-cemiyyet.html#lambs-skins

Recorded issue text

„Besser, man schert die Widder, als den Lämmern die Haut abzuziehen.“

Current local evidence

„Es ist besser, die Widder zu scheren, als den Lämmern die Haut abzuziehen.“

No further correction is recommended for the recorded issue. Retain the current verified wording and formatting.

Earlier proposal for context only

Use the parallel infinitive construction in both places: “Es ist besser, die Widder zu scheren, als den Lämmern die Haut abzuziehen.” Preserve the quotation’s meaning and its deliberate repetition.

F-072 · The empty-boat retelling does not identify its classical parallel

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

empty-boat — AZ: Bu qayıq da boşdur; EN: The empty boat; DE: Auch dieses Boot ist leer; RU: Эта лодка тоже пуста; KY: Бул кайык да бош

Issue: All five versions frame the story as an unnamed teacher’s personal experience. Its central empty-versus-occupied-boat contrast has an identifiable parallel in Zhuangzi, chapter 20. The site’s dawn meditation and first-person teacher narrative are adaptations, not the wording or scene of that passage.

Why it matters: Without an adaptation note, readers cannot trace the literary motif or distinguish the modern retelling from the classical text.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

Issue: The complete 255-title inventory contains both title case (for example The Church Bell and A Mother’s Love) and sentence case (for example A prayer for everyone and A mother's lasting lesson), without an evident genre or author distinction. Single-word and proper-name-only headings are neutral; the attached inventory includes them for coverage and does not label each title individually wrong.

Why it matters: Both styles are valid English, but alternating between them makes the same collection look inconsistently edited.

Current check: The historical inventory listed 255 English stories; the current catalog has 254. Stoning the Devil was intentionally removed. Mixed casing remains: “The Church Bell” and “A Mother’s Love” use title case, while “A prayer for everyone” and “A mother’s lasting lesson” use sentence case. The current 254 titles were read from the catalog; the historical removed entry is explicitly marked in the evidence.

F-074 · Purple-jacket tale needs a provenance note distinguishing fiction from documented custom

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

purple-jacket — AZ: Bənövşəyi çəpkən; EN: The Purple Jacket; DE: Die violette Jacke; RU: Фиолетовая куртка; KY: Кызгылт көк чепкен

Issue: All versions already frame the account as a tale. However, the Yörük Culture Research Centre at Süleyman Demirel University specifically disputes the historical custom and associates the narrative with Osman Şahin’s literary work. Other modern publications repeat the tradition claim. The competing accounts are not explained on the site.

Why it matters: A reader may still infer that the detailed communal divorce procedure is an established historical practice. Existing tale framing reduces, but does not resolve, this source ambiguity.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

Evidence and file locations (5 recorded occurrences)

az\wisdom-stories\purple-jacket.docx · az\categories\sevgi-ve-evlilik.html#purple-jacket

Current local evidence

F-076 · Inserted Hajj verse has no source or adaptation label

Needs decision Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

hajj-pilgrimage — AZ: Həcc ziyarəti; EN: The Hajj Pilgrimage; DE: Der Kampf in uns selbst; RU: Хадж; KY: Ажылык сапар

Issue: All five versions insert a four-line verse without a credit. The Azerbaijani wording closely follows a Turkish quatrain commonly attributed to Hacı Bektaş Veli in educational and cultural publications. The other versions freely adapt its final line, replacing Jerusalem and Mecca with general holy places and an explanation about inner work. This is understandable adaptation, but is not identified as such.

Why it matters: Readers cannot distinguish the borrowed verse from the site narrative or a literal translation. Modern attribution alone does not establish medieval authorship.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

Evidence and file locations (5 recorded occurrences)

AZ · hajj-pilgrimage

az\wisdom-stories\hajj-pilgrimage.docx · az\categories\iman-ve-meneviyyat.html#hajj-pilgrimage

Recorded issue text

Hərarət oddadır, sacda deyildir, Kəramət başdadır, tacda deyildir. Hər nə axtarırsansa, özündə axtar, Qüdsdə, Məkkədə, həcdə deyildir.

Current local evidence

Hərarət oddadır, sacda deyildir, Kəramət başdadır, tacda deyildir. Hər nə axtarırsansa, özündə axtar, Qüdsdə, Məkkədə, həcdə deyildir.

20 affected stories and current titles

  • another-day — EN: Another day

  • sinans-waterless-house — EN: Sinan’s waterless house
  • thoughtfulness — EN: Thoughtfulness

Issue: The complete title/body inventory finds straight internal apostrophes in 24 title or paragraph locations alongside typographic apostrophes in 573 locations. The straight forms include possessives in both headings and prose. This is typography, not a spelling or translation error.

Why it matters: Mixed apostrophe forms make the collection’s editorial presentation inconsistent.

Current check: All 24 recorded title/body replacements are present in Word, the catalog and displayed category text. However, 15 affected story articles still have straight apostrophes in both image alt text and enlargement aria-labels: 30 residual accessibility attributes in the checked category pages. This is a limited metadata follow-up, not a need to repeat the completed prose edits.

F-078 · Judge tale gives an unverified precise setting without identifying its literary parallel

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

judges-answer — AZ: Frankfurt hakiminin ədalətli hökmü; EN: The judge’s answer; DE: Das gerechte Urteil des Frankfurter Richters; RU: Справедливое решение франкфуртского судьи; KY: Франкфурт судьясынын адилет өкүмү

Issue: The tale is explicitly qualified as a legend, but specifies Frankfurt in 1506. No contemporary record for that setting was established. Hebel’s Der kluge Richter, in the 1811 Schatzkästlein, contains the same false missing-money allegation and judicial resolution, with different amounts and an unspecified Eastern setting. This proves a textual parallel, not that Hebel originated the motif or that the site directly translated him.

Why it matters: A precise place and year can suggest documentary authenticity despite the legend qualifier, while readers are given no way to trace the related literary tale.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-079 · Mercedes biography gives an unresolved remarriage-to-death interval

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

mercedes — AZ: Mercedes; EN: Mercedes; DE: Mercedes; RU: Мерседес; KY: Мерседес

Issue: The story states that Mercedes Jellinek’s second husband died a few months after their marriage. Secondary biographies disagree about the remarriage chronology; the audit has not located an independent marriage/death record resolving the interval. The supported brand dates and her death in 1929 at age thirty-nine are not being challenged.

Why it matters: An unqualified, precise biographical interval remains less secure than the surrounding manufacturer-supported history.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-081 · Respect moral leaves the staged slap unexplained

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

remarkable-lesson — AZ: Möhtəşəm dərs; EN: A Remarkable Lesson; DE: Eine eindrucksvolle Lektion; RU: Замечательный урок; KY: Таасирдүү сабак

Issue: The teacher and father arrange a demonstration in which the teacher slaps the father. The moral praises modelling respect and responsibility without distinguishing submission to a classroom rule from the physical humiliation used in the demonstration. The scene is explicitly staged; it is not a claim that the story directly instructs readers to hit anyone.

Why it matters: The central example can be read as equating respect with accepting a slap, which sits uneasily with the moral’s stated lesson about respectful conduct.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-083 · Nails parable byline does not distinguish retelling from original plot authorship

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

nails-lesson — AZ: Bir mismarın hekayəsi; EN: The nail’s lesson; DE: Die Geschichte eines Nagels; RU: История одного гвоздя; KY: Бир мыктын баяны

Issue: The story carries Etibar Siracsoy’s byline without an adaptation label. Its distinctive sequence—anger, nails driven into a fence, nails removed, remaining holes as wounds—also appears in independently published versions of the widely circulated parable. This does not establish who originated it, and is not a plagiarism allegation.

Why it matters: A bare byline can be read as credit for inventing the plot rather than for writing this particular retelling and moral.

Current check: The unqualified author byline remains. Russian and Kyrgyz Word sources spell the name in Cyrillic while the catalog/page credit uses the Latin form; neither supplies a retelling-role label. The English moral correction under F-013 is present and independent of this attribution question.

F-084 · Kyrgyz ending substitutes a stronger maxim for the source moral

Fixed Low · Translation · AZ, KY

loving-from-afar — KY: Алыстан сүйүү; AZ: Uzaqdan sevmək

Issue: The Kyrgyz moral ends “Бирок баштан өткөрүлбөгөн сүйүү да — жоготуу.” (“But love not experienced is also a loss.”). The Azerbaijani source instead explains that fear of a possible separation deprives someone of the opportunity for closeness and joy. The plot remains recognizable, but this is an interpretive adaptation. In the dialogue, the second imagined accusation also changes smiling at someone to staring at someone.

Why it matters: The comparison view presents the versions as the same story. Readers receive a more categorical concluding claim and lose the source’s explicit contrast between avoiding possible separation and missing possible closeness.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-085 · Samurai parable has an identifiable published parallel but no source note

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

unwelcome-gifts — AZ: Təhqirləri necə dəf etməli; EN: Unwelcome gifts; DE: Wie man Beleidigungen abwehrt; RU: Как отразить оскорбления; KY: Кемсинтүүгө кантип жооп берүү керек?

Issue: The samurai, provoking warrior, pupils and rejected-gift explanation closely parallel the story published on Paulo Coelho’s own blog. An older related analogy appears in the Akkosa Sutta (SN 7.2), with different characters and setting. The site labels this a samurai parable but does not identify the source used for this retelling.

Why it matters: Readers cannot distinguish the traditional motif from the modern samurai adaptation. The parallel does not establish copying, exclusive authorship, or the source actually used by the compiler.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-086 · Punctuation-loss parable needs an adaptation/source note

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

other-peoples-thoughts — AZ: Özgələrin fikirləri; EN: Other People’s Thoughts; DE: Die Gedanken der anderen; RU: Чужие мысли; KY: Өзгөлөрдүн ойлору

Issue: All five versions use the distinctive sequence of a person losing punctuation and ending with only other people’s thoughts inside quotation marks. A closely corresponding piece, «Знаки препинания», is published under Alexander Kanevsky’s name in Trud (2002) and on his Proza.ru page (2016). The site adds a separate period-loss episode and rewrites the explanations, so it is not a literal translation.

Why it matters: The absent source note prevents readers from distinguishing the published literary parallel from the compiler’s adaptation. These sources do not establish which intermediary version the compiler used.

Current check: The recorded wording or structural/content concern remains in the current local sources and site. No approved resolution was found for this finding. The issue still requires the editorial decision described here.

F-087 · Changed-water parable needs its retelling source identified

Fixed Low · Content · AZ, DE, EN, KY, RU

price-of-belonging — AZ: Çoxluq həmişə haqlı deyil; EN: The price of belonging; DE: Die Mehrheit hat nicht immer recht; RU: Большинство не всегда право; KY: Көпчүлүк дайыма эле туура боло бербейт

Issue: The warning, preserved water, isolation and eventual decision to drink the changed water closely parallel “When the Waters Were Changed” in Idries Shah’s Tales of the Dervishes. The Foundation publishes that story and its traditional attribution note. The site changes details, including the warning’s speaker and destruction of the stored water, and provides no source note.

Why it matters: Readers cannot identify the source of this particular adaptation. A published parallel does not establish exclusive authorship or the intermediary text used by the compiler.

Current check: The approved title changes under F-043 remain in place. The changed-water narrative still lacks a specific retelling-source note. Title correction and attribution are separate issues.
